L2081: ## 19.1.1. sTST
L2082: sTST was measured by Item 9 in the Sleep Diary Questionnaire, which is a daily eDiary completed in the morning between 6:00 am and 10:00 am with a recall period of “last night”.
L2083: 9. In total, how long did you sleep last night? (This should just be your best estimate, based on when you went to bed and woke up, how long it took you to fall asleep, and how long you were awake. You do not need to calculate this by adding and subtracting; just give your best estimate.)

____ h ____ min
L2084: The secondary efficacy endpoints of Study ID-078A301 and Study ID-078A302 based on sTST were defined as:
L2085: - Change from baseline to Month 1 in sTST.
- Change from baseline to Month 3 in sTST.
L2086: The Applicant has taken several approaches to explore the thresholds of clinically meaningful within-patient change in sTST.
L2087: - Patient interviews (n=17)10 people indicated that an increase of at least 2 or 3 total hours asleep to 5 nights per week would be an important improvement in treating their insomnia
- Anchor-based analyses anchored on 2 category improvement in patient global impression of severity daytime symptoms scale using phase 2 data (open-label Study AC-078A203)Approximately 1.4 weekly average sleep hours per day were estimated as clinical meaningful improvement for insomnia treatment Thresholds derived from the anchor-based analyses using phase 2 data for sTST should be confirmed using phase 3 data because of the following reasons:Open-label study design of phase 2 Study AC-078A203
- Shorter study period (2 weeks treatment) than phase 3 trials (12 weeks treatment)
- Multiple anchor scales are available in phase 3 trials
L2088: To interpret study results on sTST endpoint, anchor-based analyses using pooled phase 3 trial data were requested. The requested anchor scales to be used in anchor-based analyses include patient global impression-severity and -change (PGI-S and PGI-C) nighttime symptoms as well as PGI-S and PGI-C daytime symptoms. The derived thresholds of clinically meaningful within patient change in sTST average weekly score (in minutes) at Month 1/Visit 6 and Month 3/Visit 8 from both phase 3 trials are summarized in Table 137 and Table 138 below.

L2118: ## 19.1.2. IDSIQ
L2119: The IDSIQ v2.0 was completed by the subject each evening from 8:00 pm to 11:59 pm using an electronic diary with a recall period of “today”. The concepts elicited during the development of IDSIQ describe the subjective experience of insomnia patients across the course of a day and the impact on daytime functioning.
L2120: The conceptual framework of IDSIQ is presented in Figure 71.
L2121: **Figure 71. Conceptual Framework of IDSIQ v2.0**
L2122: Source: Applicant generated figure, IDSIQ evidence dossier, Figure 2
Abbreviations: IDSIQ, Insomnia Daytime Symptoms and Impacts Questionnaire
L2123: The IDSIQ v2.0 has 3 domains assessing Alert/Cognition, Mood, and Sleepiness. For each domain, the item responses in the respective domains are summed to provide a domain score. A total score is obtained by summing across all item responses. Prior to summation, items 1, 2, 8, 10 and 14 are reverse scored. For Sleepiness domain, Daily domain score is the sum of Item 8*, Item 11, Item 12, and Item 13; Weekly Average is the mean of the daily domain score over 7 days. The Sleepiness domain score ranges from 0 to 40 with higher score indicating greater burden of illness.
L2124: Secondary efficacy endpoints of Trial ID-078A301 and Trial ID-078A302 based on IDSIQ were defined as:
L2125: - Change from baseline to Month 1 in IDSIQ sleepiness domain score
- Change from baseline to Month 3 in IDSIQ sleepiness domain score
L2126: The Applicant has taken several approaches to explore the thresholds of clinically meaningful within patient change in IDSIQ Sleepiness domain scores.
L2127: - An exit interview study was conducted among 41 insomnia subjects exiting the phase 2 AC-078A203 study3.5 points change in Sleepiness domain using a 0 to 10 scale was reported to be clinically meaningful
- Anchor-based analyses were conducted to derive thresholds of clinical meaningful within patient change in IDSIQ v2.0 scores using phase 2 (AC-078A203) data.
L2128: clinical meaningful improvement is typically considered to have at least 2-point improvement in PGI-S and “moderately better” in PGI-C.
L2129: - Thresholds derived from the anchor-based analyses using phase 2 data for IDSIQ are not appropriate because of the following reasons:Open-label study design of phase 2 Study AC-078A203
- Shorter study period (2 weeks treatment) than phase 3 trials (12 weeks treatment)
L2130: To interpret study results on IDSIQ Sleep domain endpoint, anchor-based analyses using pooled phase 3 trial data were requested. The requested anchor scales to be used in anchor-based analyses include PGI-S and PGI-C nighttime symptoms as well as PGI-S and PGI-C daytime symptoms. The derived thresholds of clinical meaningful within patient change in IDSIQ Sleepiness domain score at Month 1/Visit 6 and Month 3/Visit 8 from both phase 3 trials were summarized in Table 139 and Table 140.

L2151: The eCDF plots by treatment arm for Trial ID-078A301 showed that there was a clear separation between 50 mg treatment arm and placebo arm within the range of thresholds of clinical meaningful improvement (from -5.1 to -10.1 from baseline to Month 1 and from -6.6 to -10.9 from baseline to Month 3) in IDSIQ Sleepiness domain score at both Month 1 and Month 3. However, there appears no separation between 25 mg treatment arm and placebo arm within these ranges of thresholds at neither Month 1 nor Month 3.
L2152: The eCDF plots by treatment arm for Trial ID-078A302 showed that there was a small separation of 25 mg treatment arm from placebo arm within the range of thresholds of clinical meaningful improvement (from -5.1 to -10.1 from baseline to Month 1 and from -6.6 to -10.9 from baseline to Month 3) in IDSIQ Sleepiness domain score at Month 1 and Month 3.
L2153: The anchor-based analyses results described above are consistent with the statistical analysis results Trial ID-078A301 and Trial ID-078A302 below:
L2154: Trial ID-078A301 results on IDSIQ sleepiness domain showed that:
L2155: - Changes from baseline were statistically significant at Month 1 and Month 3 for daridorexant 50 mg compared to placebo.
- For daridorexant 25 mg compared to placebo, a numerical trend for improvement was observed at Month 1 and Month 3 but changes were not statistically significant.
L2156: Trial ID-078A302 results on IDSIQ sleepiness domain showed that:
L2157: - For daridorexant 25 mg, differences to placebo were not statistically significant at Month 1 or Month 3 (two-sided p-values 0.0733 and 0.0120, respectively)
L2158: ## 20. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L2159: The clinical investigators (CIs), Drs. Gotfried, Heller, Ahmad, and Thein were inspected in support of this original New Drug Application (NDA 214985) for daridorexant for the treatment of insomnia (Protocols ID-078A301 and ID-078A302).
L2160: Two complaints against Dr. Heller were investigated for Study ID-078A301 (Site #3575) and no evidence of subject safety or data integrity issues was identified to support the allegations. In response to an Agency request, the Applicant provided re-analysis of efficacy for all endpoints included in the Type I error control scheme for Study 301 with the data from Site # 3575 excluded. Statistical significance remained the same for all endpoints and exclusion of the results from Site #3575 did not alter conclusions regarding efficacy.
L2161: Overall, based on the results of these inspections, the conduct of the above studies was adequate, and the clinical data generated from these CI sites appear to be reliable in support of this NDA.

Applicant proposed language.	5.2 Worsening of Depression/Suicidal Ideation
The Applicant proposed "Worsening of Depression/Suicidal Ideation" as Section 5.3. The language was moved to Section 5.2 and revised to:
Patients with psychiatric disorders, including insomnia, are at increased risk of suicide. In primarily depressed patients treated with hypnotics, worsening of depression and suicidal thoughts and actions (including completed suicides) have been reported. As with other hypnotics, QUVIVIQ should be administered with caution in patients exhibiting symptoms of depression. Monitoring of suicide risk and protective measures may be required.

L2168: SECTION	PROPOSED LABELING	REVISED LABELING
5.4 Patients with Compromised Respiratory Function
Applicant proposed language stating that QUVIVIQ(b)(4) [REDACTED]
5.4 Complex Sleep Behaviors
Applicant's proposed "Patients with Compromised Respiratory Function" as Section 5.4 was moved to Section 5.5.
Applicant did not include a Warning and Precaution for Complex Sleep Behaviors. The following language was added as Section 5.4:
Complex sleep behaviors, including sleep-walking, sleep-driving, and engaging in other activities while not fully awake (e.g., preparing and eating food, making phone calls, having sex), have been reported to occur with the use of hypnotics including orexin(b)(4) receptor antagonists such as QUVIVIQ. These events can occur in hypnotic-naïve as well as in hypnotic-experienced persons. Patients usually do not remember these events. Complex sleep behaviors may occur following the first or any subsequent use of QUVIVIQ, with or without the concomitant use of alcohol and other CNS depressants [see Drug Interactions (7.1)]. Discontinue QUVIVIQ immediately if a patient experiences a complex sleep behavior.
L2169: SECTION	PROPOSED LABELING	REVISED LABELING
5.5 Patients with Compromised Respiratory Function
The Applicant proposed "Patients with Compromised Respiratory Function" as Section 5.4. The warning and precaution was moved to Section 5.5 and revised to state that QUVIVIQ was not studied in moderate OSA requiring CPAP, severe OSA, or severe COPD. The section recommends that HCPs consider respiratory function if prescribed to patients with compromised respiratory function and includes a reference to Section 8.7 Patients with Compromised Respiratory Function.

Applicant did not propose a Warning and Precaution titled "Need to Evaluate for Comorbid Diagnoses."	
5.6 Need to Evaluate for Comorbid Diagnoses
The following language was added as Section 5.6 to align with other drugs in the class:
Because sleep disturbances may be the presenting manifestation of a medical and/or psychiatric disorder, treatment of insomnia should be initiated only after careful evaluation of the patient. The failure of insomnia to remit after 7 to 10 days of treatment may indicate the presence of a primary psychiatric and/or medical illness that should be evaluated. Worsening of insomnia or the emergence of new cognitive or behavioral abnormalities may be the result of an unrecognized underlying psychiatric or medical disorder and can emerge during the course of treatment with sleep-promoting drugs such as QUVIVIQ.

L2175: ## 22. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2176: The Division consulted the Division of Pediatric and Maternal Health (DPMH) for the review of this application. Daridorexant is indicated for a condition that would be expected to be seen in females of reproductive potential, during pregnancy, and during lactation. Therefore, DPMH recommends postmarketing requirements for the applicant to conduct a pregnancy exposure
L2177: registry, a complimentary study of a different design, and clinical lactation study. They are as follows:
L2178: ## **Post Marketing Requirement (PMR) 4150-1**
L2179: - Conduct a lactation study in lactating women who have received therapeutic doses of daridorexant using a validated assay to assess concentrations of daridorexant in breast milk using a validated assay (b) (4).
L2180: ## **PMR 4150-2**
L2181: - Conduct a prospective, registry based observational exposure cohort study that compares the maternal, fetal, and infant outcomes of women exposed to daridorexant during pregnancy to an unexposed control population. The registry will detect and record major and minor congenital malformations, spontaneous abortions, stillbirths, elective terminations, small for gestational age, preterm birth, and any other adverse pregnancy outcomes. These outcomes will be assessed throughout pregnancy. Infant outcomes, including effects on postnatal growth and development, will be assessed through at least the first year of life.
L2182: ## **PMR 4150-3**
L2183: - Conduct an additional pregnancy study that uses a different design from the pregnancy registry (for example a case control study or a retrospective cohort study using claims or electronic medical record data) to assess major congenital malformations, spontaneous abortions, stillbirths, and small for gestational age and preterm birth in women exposed to daridorexant during pregnancy compared to an unexposed control population.
L2184: Efficacy data for daridorexant indicate reduced WASO with 25 and 50 mg bedtime doses. However, it is important for persons to be arousable from sleep in cases of emergency, and once aroused to be able to function. Adverse events of dizziness and impairments in balance and coordination, and, to a lesser extent, cognition were common and observed at rates higher than placebo in a dose dependent manner. Thus, assessment of effects on balance and cognition after arousal from sleep are also warranted. It is useful to patients and clinicians to know what levels of impairment in arousability &amp; post-middle-of-night arousal psychomotor function should be expected from use of this drug. Such information will enhance safety-informed decision-making regarding its use. The following PMR is recommended to provide the aforementioned information:
L2185: ## **PMR 4150-4**
L2186: - Middle of the Night Safety Study in Female and Male patients or healthy controls aged 18 to 65 and &gt;65 years to assess the ability to awaken to sound in the middle of the night and postural stability and cognitive function following awakening.
L2187: Given that pediatric patients also experience insomnia, the Applicant proposed studies in the iPSP and has agreed to conduct the following studies in patients aged 10 to &lt;18 years old:
L2188: ## **Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA) PMR 4150-5**
L2189: - Dose-finding (b) (4) study
L2190: ## **PREA PMR 4150-6**
L2191: - Pediatric effectiveness and safety (b) (4) study
L2192: ## **PREA PMR 4150-7**
L2193: - (b) (4)
L2194: The Applicant has requested a waiver of pediatric assessments in children below 10 years of age. This is because assessing sleep in children below 10 years of age is complex and challenging, due to the high variability of sleep and napping time, both across the age range, and between children of the same age.

L2286: ## 1. Executive Summary
L2287: The applicant, Idorsia Pharmaceuticals, Ltd., submitted the results from the human abuse potential study ID-078-107 for the assessment of abuse potential of ACT-541468 (Name of finished product: Daridorexant).
L2288: Study ID-078-107 was a Phase 1 randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, placebo- and active-controlled, 6-way cross-over study to evaluate the abuse potential of single oral doses of ACT-541468 in healthy recreational drug users. The primary objective was to determine the abuse potential of ACT-541468 using placebo as control and suvorexant and zolpidem as active controls (positive controls). The study consisted of a 3-period Qualification Phase and a 6-period Core Phase (Treatment Phase).
L2289: There were six treatments in the Core Phase. These treatments were 30 mg zolpidem, 150 mg suvorexant, 50, 100 and 150 mg doses of ACT-541468, and placebo. In the Core Phase, 72 subjects were randomized, 71 subjects received at least one dose of study drug, and 63 subjects (87.5%) completed this phase of the study. Subjects discontinued in the Core Phase primarily for other reasons (5 subjects [6.9%]), such as family matters, while 3 subjects (4.2%) withdrew voluntarily, and 1 subject (1.4%) discontinued due to AEs.
L2290: The results from the reviewer's primary analysis on Drug Liking Emax and secondary analyses on Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax showed that 100 and 150 mg doses of ACT-541468 did not produce significantly smaller subject responses compared to both 30 mg zolpidem and 150 mg suvorexant (p\ge 0.1955), and 50 mg ACT-541468 produced significantly smaller subject responses than both positive control drugs (p\le 0.046) except the comparison with 30 mg zolpidem for Take Drug Again Emax (p=0.1562). The exploratory comparisons were performed between 50 mg ACT-541468 and placebo for Drug Liking Emax and Overall Drug Liking Emax. The results showed that 50 mg ACT-541468 produced significantly greater subject responses than placebo (p\le 0.0215). The study was validated by the significant difference between each positive control and placebo for Drug Liking Emax with a test margin of 15 (p=0.0003).
L2291: The reviewer conducted sensitivity analyses on Drug Liking Emax, Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax based on the modified completer population (MCP). The results from the sensitivity analyses were consistent of the findings from both primary and secondary analyses.
L2292: For other secondary endpoints: Emin scores of Alertness/Drowsiness and Observer's Assessment of Alertness/Sedation (composite score and sum score), Emax scores of Any Effects, Bad Effects, Good Effects, High, and Composite Score of Bowdle (external perception and internal perception), as well as Drug Similarity VAS the descriptive statistics are provided in Section 2.4.4.
L2293: In conclusion, the results from the HAP study ID-078-107 show that abuse potential of ACT-541468 is similar to those of zolpidem and suvorexant.
L2294: ## 2. Review report on Study SEP360-101
L2295: ## 2.1. Overview
L2296: This was a Phase 1 randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, placebo- and active-controlled, 6-way cross-over study to evaluate the abuse potential of single oral doses of ACT-541468 in healthy recreational drug users.
L2297: ## 2.1.1. Objectives of the study
L2298: Primary Objective:
L2299: - To determine the abuse potential of ACT-541468 using placebo as control, and suvorexant and zolpidem as active comparators.
L2300: Secondary Objectives:
L2301: - To evaluate the safety and tolerability of ACT-541468 at therapeutic and supra-therapeutic single doses.
- To assess the pharmacokinetics (PK) of single oral doses of 50, 100, and 150 mg ACT-541468.
- To investigate the PK/pharmacodynamic (PD) relationship between ACT-541468 plasma concentrations (and/or PK parameters) and measures of abuse potential.
L2302: ## 2.1.2. Study design
L2303: This was a Phase 1 randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, placebo- and active-controlled, 6-way cross-over study to evaluate the abuse potential of single oral doses of ACT-541468 in healthy recreational drug users.
L2304: The study consisted of a 3-period qualification phase (Part A) and a 6-period core phase (Part B). Each period started at the time of first administration (i.e., Day 1) and ended on the day of the last assessment related to the administration schedule. In each period of the core phase, a single-dose treatment was administered (ACT-541468, suvorexant, zolpidem, or placebo). The maximum total study duration per subject was approximately 9 weeks (from screening to End-of-Study [EOS]).
L2305: Schematic overviews of the qualification and core phases are given in Figure 9-1 and Figure 9-2, respectively, in the study report.
L2306: Figure 9-1 Study design: qualification phase (Part A)
L2307: Suvorexant: 150 mg; zolpidem: 30 mg
L2308: Figure 9-2 Study design: core phase (Part B)
L2309: ACT-541468: 50 mg, 100 mg, 150 mg; suvorexant: 150 mg; zolpidem: 30 mg.
L2310: ## 2.1.3. Disposition of Subjects
L2311: A total of 72 subjects were randomized to the Core Phase and 71 subjects received at least 1 dose of study drug, and 63 (87%) subjects completed this phase of the study. Nine subjects (12.5%) discontinued prior to study completion, including 1 subject who was randomized but did not receive any dose of study drug in the core phase and 8 subjects who discontinued after receiving at least one dose of study drug. These subjects discontinued primarily for other reasons (5 subjects [6.9%]), such as family matters, while 3 subjects (4.2%) withdrew voluntarily, and 1 subject (1.4%) discontinued due to AEs.
L2312: ## 2.1.4. Abuse potential endpoints
L2313: ## Primary endpoint
L2314: The Emax of the Drug Liking VAS ('at this moment') assessed repeatedly over 24 h post-dose during each treatment period was used as primary endpoint in this study. It is a bipolar VAS scale ranging from 0 (i.e., maximum disliking) to 100 points (i.e., maximum liking) with 50 points representing a neutral response.
L2315: ## Secondary endpoint
L2316: The following secondary PD endpoints were applied to assess the balance of effects as well as positive/negative, sedative, psychedelic, and other effects. In addition, some objective PD endpoints were applied.
L2317: ## *Balance of effects*
L2318: - Drug Liking VAS (bipolar): TEmax, Emin, TEmin, TA_AUE
- Overall Drug Liking VAS (bipolar): Emax, Emin
- Take Drug Again VAS (bipolar): Emax, Emin
L2319: ## *Positive Effects*
L2320: - Good Effects VAS (unipolar): Emax, TEmax, TA_AUE
- Feeling High VAS (unipolar): Emax, TEmax, TA_AUE
L2321: ## *Negative Effects*
L2322: - Bad Effects VAS (unipolar): Emax, TEmax, TA_AUE
L2323: ## *Sedative and Other Effects*
L2324: - Alertness/Drowsiness VAS (bipolar): Emin, TEmin, time-averaged area over the effect
L2325: curve (TA_AOE)
L2326: - Observer's Assessment of Alertness/Sedation (OAA/S) composite and sum scores (unipolar): Emin, TEmin, TA_AOE
L2327: ## *Psychedelic effects*
L2328: - Bowdle VAS (unipolar): Emax for Internal and External Perceptions composite scores
L2329: ## *Other effects*
L2330: - Drug Similarity VAS (unipolar): 12 h score
- Any Effects VAS (unipolar): Emax, TEmax, TA_AUE
L2331: ## *Objective PD endpoints*
L2332: - Reaction time task (RTI) median five-choice reaction time: Emax, area under the effect time curve (AUE)
- RTI median five-choice error score: Emax, AUE
- Paired Associates Learning (PAL): total errors adjusted Emax, AUE; total attempts Emax, AUE; first attempt memory score Emin
- Rapid visual information processing (RVP) target sensitivity (A' prime): Emin
- RVP median response latency: Emax, AUE
L2333: ## 2.1.5. Statistical methodologies used in the Sponsor's analyses
L2334: The statistical hypotheses used were recommended in FDA 2017 Guidance. The test margins for the primary endpoint were prespecified at 15, 0, and 11 for the comparisons positive control versus placebo, positive control versus test drug, and test drug versus placebo, respectively. The significance level for each test was 0.05. Analyses for secondary and exploratory endpoints used one-sided tests for the comparisons between positive control and placebo, and between positive control and test drug at the significance level of 0.05. For the comparison between test drug and placebo, the two-sided test with the significance level of 0.10 was used. All tests for the secondary endpoints and exploratory endpoints are with a margin zero.
L2335: Analysis of primary and secondary endpoints were performed using a mixed-effect ANOVA model. The model was to include treatment, period, and treatment sequence as fixed effects, and baseline (pre-dose) measurement as a covariate where applicable, and subject nested within sequence as a random effect. A first-order direct carry-over effect from the treatment of the previous period was included in the model if it was found significant at 25% level. For the first treatment period, treatment 'A' was used for all subjects and all PD endpoints. From each model, least squares means and CIs were provided for each treatment; and difference in least squares mean, CIs of the difference, and p-values were provided for each treatment comparison.
L2336: The assumption of normal distribution of the data required for the ANOVA model was examined using a Shapiro-Wilk W normality test on the residuals from the mixed-effect model. Parameters were analyzed under the assumption of a normal distribution if the p-value of the test was \ge 0.05. Based on the Shapiro-Wilk W test, the model was decided through the steps described in Section 9.8.3 (note that this was a change to the planned analysis in the SAP).
L2337: Reviewer's comments: The sponsor stated in Section 9.8.3. that the t-test was only used if the distribution of the paired difference was normal or slightly skewed, i.e., upper-tailed = 0 to 0.5; lower-tailed = -0.5 to 0; two-tailed = -0.5 to 0.5.
L2338: If the probability value was &lt;0.05 for the Shapiro Wilk W test, and the residuals appeared to be severely non-normal in distribution and the paired differences between treatments did not graphically appear to be moderately symmetric (are heavily skewed), then the endpoint was analyzed non-parametrically. The Sign test and the distribution-free confidence interval (CI) were used to evaluate treatment differences.
L2339: Reviewer's comments: In the Section 9.8.3. the sponsor stated:
L2340: For inferential analyses, if the result from the CI did not match the result from the p-value, i.e., the margin was not contained in the CI but the p-value was not significant, the CI and the p-value were set to 'not applicable'.
L2341: The confidence interval cannot always be used for making a conclusion for a statistical test, especially the Sign test and the distribution free CI are two different methodologies. The conclusion of a comparison should be based on the Sign test.
L2342: The sponsor's post-hoc analyses are also included in the Section 9.8.3.
L2343: ## 2.1.6. Sponsor's summary of the pharmacodynamic evaluation and conclusions
L2344: The sponsor's pharmacodynamic results
L2345: - Did the positive control drugs produce mean responses that showed greater abuse potential compared to placebo in order to claim study validity (hypothesis #1)?

The positive comparators (150 mg suvorexant and 30 mg zolpidem) produced responses that showed greater drug liking compared to placebo:

150 mg suvorexant was associated with a difference of 29.0 compared to placebo, with a lower bound of the CI of 26.0 (p &lt; 0.0001), which was greater than the pre-specified margin of 15.
- 30 mg zolpidem showed a difference of 26.0 compared to placebo, with a lower bound of the CI of 22.0 (p &lt; 0.0001), which was greater than the pre-specified margin of 15.
These data demonstrate that the overall study can be considered valid according to the pre-specified criteria.

- Did the test drug ACT-541468 produce mean responses that showed less abuse potential compared to the positive control drugs (hypothesis #2)?

The test drug (ACT-541468) did not produce responses that showed less drug liking compared to the positive comparators at all dose levels:

150 mg suvorexant and 30 mg zolpidem – 50 mg ACT-541468 differences (lower bound of the CI) were 7.5 (4.1) and 6.6 (3.3), respectively (p \le 0.0007).
- 150 mg suvorexant and 30 mg zolpidem – 100 mg ACT-541468 differences (lower bound of the CI) were 3.0 (-1.0) and 1.0 (-2.0), respectively (p \ge 0.1958).
- 150 mg suvorexant and 30 mg zolpidem – 150 mg ACT-541468 differences (lower bound of the CI) were -0.6 (-2.7) and -3.0 (-4.0), respectively (p \ge 0.6583).
L2346: Thus, ACT-541468 showed significantly less drug-liking at the 50 mg dose compared to both positive comparators, but not at the doses of 100 and 150 mg.

3. Did the test drug ACT-541468 produce mean responses that show similar abuse potential compared to placebo (hypothesis #3)?

As the test drug ACT-541468 was significantly different from both positive comparators only at the dose of 50 mg, but not at doses of 100 or 150 mg (see hypothesis #2), the p-values for comparisons between ACT-541468 and placebo are not shown. However, descriptive results showed differences and upper bounds of the CI that exceeded the pre-specified margin of 11, indicating that ACT-541468 did not produce responses that were similar to placebo.
L2347: ## *Time course of Drug Liking effects*
L2348: Suvorexant, zolpidem, and ACT-541468 showed a similar time to peak effects (i.e., TEmax) typically occurring at 1.5 to 2 h post-dose. However, the duration of PD effects was slightly longer with suvorexant compared to zolpidem and all doses of ACT-541468, i.e., generally returning to baseline by 12 h post-dose for suvorexant vs 6–8 h post-dose for zolpidem and ACT-541468, respectively.
L2349: ## *Secondary PD endpoints*
L2350: Overall Drug Liking VAS, Take Drug Again VAS, Feeling High VAS, Good Effects VAS, Bad Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, Any Effects VAS
L2351: Both suvorexant and zolpidem significantly differentiated from placebo with respect to each of these 7 VAS assessments. The 50 mg dose of ACT-541468 consistently showed scores that were significantly different from both suvorexant and zolpidem in favor of ACT-541468 50 mg. This was applicable to each of these PD VAS assessments except for the Take Drug Again VAS in comparison to zolpidem. However, no significant differences from comparator data were observed at the doses of 100 mg and 150 mg ACT-541468 for the majority of assessments.
L2352: ## *Bowdle VAS for assessment of perceptual effects*
L2353: Both suvorexant and zolpidem significantly differentiated from placebo with respect to Internal and External Perception composite scales. ACT-541468 showed significantly lower scores (i.e., less alterations of internal and external perception) compared to zolpidem at all doses and compared to suvorexant at the 50 mg and 100 mg doses. All doses of ACT-541468 differentiated from placebo.
L2354: ## *Drug Similarity VAS*
L2355: Based on Drug Similarity VAS data, the perceived effects of zolpidem, suvorexant, and ACT-541468 were consistently considered more similar to the effects of benzodiazepines and other sedatives than those of other psychoactive substances such as opioids and cannabis. These VAS values with respect to benzodiazepines and other sedatives were lowest at the 50 mg dose of ACT-541468 as compared to all other active treatments.
L2356: ## *Observer's Assessment of Alertness/Sedation*
L2357: Both suvorexant and zolpidem differentiated from placebo based on composite and sum scores of the OAA/S, i.e., indicating sedative effects greater than placebo. The lowest degree of sedation was
L2358: determined for the 50 mg dose of ACT-541468 as compared to all other active treatments. All doses of ACT-541468 differentiated from placebo.
L2359: ## *Objective assessments of cognitive function (reaction time task, Rapid Visual Information Processing, Paired Associates Learning score)*
L2360: Both suvorexant and zolpidem showed significant cognitive impairment with respect to the majority of endpoints, including RTI – five-choice reaction time, RVP – response latency, and PAL – total errors. Compared to zolpidem, ACT-541468 showed significantly less impairment at all doses with respect to these 3 endpoints. Compared to suvorexant, less impairment was noted for the 50 mg dose of ACT-541468. The doses of 100 mg and 150 mg ACT-541468 both differentiated from placebo, whereas at the 50 mg dose, this was only applicable for RVP – response latency.
L2361: ## Conclusion
L2362: - Study validity was achieved for both comparators, zolpidem and suvorexant, since the difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between each positive comparator drug and placebo exceeded the pre-defined margin of 15 points.
- The primary endpoint analysis revealed a significantly lower Drug Liking VAS Emax of ACT-541468 compared to both suvorexant and zolpidem at 50 mg (i.e., highest dose in phase 3). No significant differences were determined at 100 and 150 mg.
- ACT-541468 showed lower effects on the majority of secondary endpoints (i.e., balance of effects, positive, negative, sedative, psychedelic, and any effects) compared to both suvorexant and zolpidem at the dose of 50 mg, but not at 100 mg or 150 mg.
- Equivalence of Drug Liking VAS Emax to placebo was not determined for any dose of ACT-541468.
- There was no PK/PD association between Cmax and Drug Liking VAS Emax after administration of ACT-541468.

L2387: ## 2.4.1.2. Statistical Testing
L2388: For the primary endpoint (1) at least one positive control must demonstrate significantly larger mean responses than placebo with a margin 15 to validate the study; (2) all doses of ACT-541468 must demonstrate significantly smaller mean responses than the validated positive control(s) to show that ACT-541468 produces less drug liking than positive control(s); (3) all doses of ACT-541468 must demonstrate the difference from placebo significantly smaller than 11 to show that ACT-541468 produces drug liking similar to placebo. In the sequential testing, an insignificant test will result in cessation of testing the following hypothesis. Therefore, no multiplicity adjustment for Type I error rate is required, and the significance level of each test is 5%. Figure 3 is the testing procedure.
L2389: **Figure 3: Testing procedure for the primary endpoint**
L2390: Note: \mu_{C1} and \mu_{C2} denote means produced by 30 mg zolpidem and 150 mg suvorexant, respectively, and \mu_{T50}, \mu_{T100} and \mu_{T150} denote means produced by 50, 100, and 150 mg doses of ACT-541468. S and NS denote the test is significant or not, respectively.
L2391: In the reviewer's analysis, when any dose of ACT-541468 passed the comparison with both positive controls, the comparison between this dose of ACT-541468 and placebo was performed regardless of the significance in the comparisons between other doses of ACT-541468 and the positive controls. However, the result from this comparison can only be used for exploratory purpose. For decision making, one can pursue that ACT-541468 has similar abuse potential to placebo if and only if all doses of ACT-541468 passed the comparisons with all validated positive controls.
L2392: The statistical model used in the reviewer's primary analysis was a mixed-effects model which included sequence, period, treatment and first-order-carryover effect as fixed effects, and subject as a random effect. For the primary endpoint, the W test on the residuals was significant (p=0.0072). Therefore, the normality of paired differences was further examined. Table 2 shows p-value of the W test and skewness for each paired difference for Drug Liking Emax.

L2433: ## 2.4.3. Sensitivity Analyses
L2434: Currently we recommend all statistical analyses in a HAP study being conducted on a Modified Completer Population (MCP). The recommended MCP is defined as completers who do not satisfy any of the following two criteria for the primary endpoint Drug Liking Emax:
L2435: - The difference between maximum and minimum of Emax scores for all treatments is smaller than or equal to 5 (that is, similar Emax scores across all treatments including placebo).
- Emax(P) &gt; 60 and Emax(P) - Emax(Z30) \ge 5 (or Emax(P) - Emax(S150) \ge 5).
L2436: Because the analyses in this study were prespecified to use the Completer Population. The reviewer's sensitivity analyses were based on the MCP.
L2437: By examining the data, five subjects (# [REDACTED](b) (6)) did not belong to MCP. Therefore, the sensitivity analyses on Drug Liking Emax, Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax were based on 58 subjects. Table 7 shows the means and standard errors for three endpoints in the sensitivity analyses.

L2470: Drug Similarity VAS is a unipolar scale (0: definitely not, 100: definitely so). The data for Drug Similarity VAS were collected at hour 12 post-dose. Drugs in the comparisons with each treatment in the study were amphetamine, barbiturates, benzodiazepines, cannabis, cocaine, ecstasy, ketamine, lysergic acid diethylamide, methamphetamine, opioids, phencyclidine, psilocybin, and sedatives. Table 13 shows summary statistics for subjects' responses to the comparisons between each treatment and each drug. The N denotes the number of subjects who gave a response for such a comparison. For example, compared to amphetamine 12 subjects gave responses to all treatments. Compared to barbiturates only one subject gave a zero response to all treatments. Because 0 denotes “definitely not”, the N for each treatment compared to each drug should be 63. It is not clear to this reviewer, how the investigator instructed subjects to respond to questionnaires for Drug Similarity VAS, and why Ns are not all equal to 63. Nevertheless, almost all subjects responded to all treatments when compared to benzodiazepines. Because the Ns may be different, drug to drug comparisons for these summary statistics for a treatment is not meaningful. For example, only one subject compared 150 mg suvorexant to ketamine with a similarity score 84, and the mean score is 84 (84/1). Comparing 84 to the mean similarity score of 61.8 based on 63 subjects who compared 150 mg suvorexant to benzodiazepines is not meaningful.

L2475: ## 3. Conclusion
L2476: The results from the reviewer's primary analysis on Drug Liking Emax showed that 100 and 150 mg doses of ACT-541468 did not produce less drug liking compared to both 30 mg zolpidem and 150 mg suvorexant. The results from secondary analyses on Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax supported the primary findings, and showed that compared to both positive controls, overall, subjects did not like 100 and 150 mg doses of ACT-541468 less and did not have less interest to take 100 mg and 150 mg doses of ACT-541468 again. In conclusion, the results from the HAP study ID-078-107 showed that abuse potential of ACT-541468 was similar to those of zolpidem and suvorexant.

L2482: Diagram illustrating the Hypothesis Testing Strategy, Trials ID-078A301 and ID-078A302. The diagram shows four rows of hypotheses, each corresponding to a time point (Month 1, Month 3, Month 1, Month 3) and a dose comparison (High dose vs placebo, Low dose vs placebo). Each row contains four hypotheses (H1, H2, H3, H4) comparing placebo (P) against three active treatments (STST, DSIO, WASO). The hypotheses are connected by arrows indicating relationships and associated weights (fractions).
The overall significance level is \alpha, which is split into \alpha/2 for the top row and \alpha/2 for the bottom row. The top row (High dose vs placebo) is associated with a Bonferroni correction followed by a gatekeeping procedure.
The hypotheses are grouped into four rows:
Row 1 (High dose vs placebo; Month 1): H1_PPS, H1_STST, H1_DSIO, H1_WASO.
Row 2 (High dose vs placebo; Month 3): H2_PPS, H2_STST, H2_DSIO, H2_WASO.
Row 3 (Low dose vs placebo; Month 1): H3_PPS, H3_STST, H3_DSIO, H3_WASO.
Row 4 (Low dose vs placebo; Month 3): H4_PPS, H4_STST, H4_DSIO, H4_WASO.
The connections between hypotheses are defined by weights (fractions) on the arrows:
Vertical connections (H1 to H2, H2 to H3, H3 to H4): 1/2.
Diagonal connections (H1 to H3, H3 to H1, H2 to H4, H4 to H2): 1/4.
Horizontal connections (H1 to H4, H4 to H1): 1.

The interacting compounds and their effects are:

Moderate CYP3A4 Inhibitor: Diltiazem (240 mg). The AUC is slightly increased (mean effect around 1.15), and the 90% CI crosses the reference line.
Moderate CYP3A4 Inducer: Efavirenz (600 mg). The AUC is slightly decreased (mean effect around 0.95), and the 90% CI crosses the reference line.
Gastric pH Modifier: Famotidine (40 mg). The AUC is slightly decreased (mean effect around 0.95), and the 90% CI crosses the reference line.
Alcohol: The AUC is slightly decreased (mean effect around 0.95), and the 90% CI crosses the reference line.
SSRI: Citalopram (20 mg). The AUC is slightly decreased (mean effect around 0.95), and the 90% CI crosses the reference line.
Legend: • AUC, □ C_{max}, * predicted.
Source: Applicant proposed label, fig-1.

L2502: Chemical structure diagram illustrating the Daridorexant Metabolic Pathway and Enzymes Involved. The diagram shows the conversion of Daridorexant (M5) into various metabolites (M1, M3, M4, M10, M12, M13, M24) via cytochrome P450 enzymes (CYP1A2, CYP2C19, CYP2C8, CYP2D6, CYP2C9, CYP3A4, CYP2C19). M5 is converted to M4 (via CYP2C19), M12 (via CYP3A4/CYP2C19), and M2 (via CYP3A4/CYP2C19). M5 is also converted to M3 (via CYP3A4/CYP2C19). M3 is converted to M10 (via CYP3A4) and M1 (via CYP2C19). M1 is converted to M24 (via CYP3A4/CYP2C19). M24 is converted to M13 (via CYP3A4/CYP2C19). M10 is also shown to be formed via CYP3A4/CYP2C19.

L2536: Empirical Cumulative Distribution Function (eCDF) plot showing the percentage of subjects versus the change from Month 3 to Run-out in WASO (min). The x-axis ranges from -300 to 300 minutes, and the y-axis ranges from 0 to 100%. Three curves are plotted: Daridorexant 25 mg (solid red line, N=310, n=286, 92.3%), Daridorexant 50 mg (dashed green line, N=310, n=284, 91.6%), and Placebo (dotted black line, N=310, n=282, 91.0%). The curves show that the Daridorexant groups generally exhibit a greater reduction in WASO compared to the Placebo group, with the 25 mg dose showing the steepest slope, indicating a higher percentage of subjects achieving a greater reduction in WASO.

L2541: Flowchart illustrating the Hypothesis Testing Strategy:
Assay Sensitivity on Day 2 (at \alpha = 0.05) leads to Day 2 analysis.
Assay Sensitivity on Day 5 (at \alpha = 0.05) leads to Day 5 analysis.
Day 2 analysis (\alpha = 0.025) leads to ACT-541468 50 mg vs. clinically relevant threshold of 2.6 cm.
Day 5 analysis (\alpha = 0.025) leads to ACT-541468 50 mg vs. clinically relevant threshold of 2.6 cm.
ACT-541468 50 mg vs. clinically relevant threshold of 2.6 cm (\alpha = 0.025) leads to ACT-541468 100 mg vs. clinically relevant threshold of 2.6 cm.
ACT-541468 50 mg vs. clinically relevant threshold of 2.6 cm (\alpha = 0.025) leads to ACT-541468 100 mg vs. clinically relevant threshold of 2.6 cm.

L2560: Figure 2: A line graph showing the mean time course profiles for Drug Liking VAS by treatment (N=63). The X-axis is Time (hr) from 0 to 24. The Y-axis is Mean response from 40 to 100. Six treatments are compared: Z30 (blue circles), S150 (orange circles), T50 (gray circles), T100 (yellow circles), T150 (light blue circles), and P (green circles). All treatments start around 50-60 mean response at time 0. Z30 and S150 show the highest initial responses, peaking around 75-80 at 2-4 hours, and then decreasing. T50, T100, and T150 show intermediate responses, peaking around 65-70. P shows the lowest response, remaining around 50-55. By 24 hours, all treatments converge to a mean response between 50 and 55.
L2561: Figure 3: Flowchart illustrating the testing procedure for the primary endpoint. The procedure starts with two tests: H_0: mu_S150 - mu_P <= 15 and H_0: mu_Z30 - mu_P <= 15. If both are Not Significant (NS), the study fails. If either is Significant (S), the procedure continues. If both are S, the next step is to compare Z30 to S150 (H_0: mu_Z30 - mu_S150 <= 0). If this test is S, the procedure proceeds to three tests comparing T doses to Z30: H_0: mu_T50 - mu_Z30 <= 0, H_0: mu_T100 - mu_Z30 <= 0, and H_0: mu_T150 - mu_Z30 <= 0. If all three are S, the study concludes that the mean produced by T is smaller than that by Z30. If any of the three tests is NS, the procedure proceeds to three tests comparing T doses to placebo: H_0: mu_T50 - mu_P <= 11, H_0: mu_T100 - mu_P <= 11, and H_0: mu_T150 - mu_P <= 11. If all three are S, the study concludes that the mean produced by T is similar to that by placebo. If any of these tests is NS, the study fails.
